From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF43.1, F43.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because... ever since my father-in-law moved in with us, things have just been a bit off. I mean, I’ve been holding it together fine at work, but at home and with my coursework, I’m just a lot more wound up and short-tempered than I’m used to being. \n\nAnd I guess I’ve started skipping the things that usually keep my head clear. I haven't been going fishing, or doing my woodworking, or even attending those first-aid workshops I usually like. I've just been skipping them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty. It's not like that. It's more just... frustration, I guess. I just feel constantly on edge and irritated, but not down in the dumps or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any anxiety or worry since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't call it anxiety. I'm not really worrying about anything specific, or feeling nervous. Like I said, it's just this constant irritation. I'm just wound up and short-tempered, not anxious.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding your father-in-law or certain situations involving him?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not avoiding him or anything. We still eat dinner together and sit in the same room. I'm just... easily annoyed when he's around, but I don't go out of my way to stay away from him.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do when you are doing them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I still enjoy doing the things I usually like to do. I mean, my hobbies and all that, they're still fun for me. I haven't lost interest in anything. I mean, my job as an EMT can be stressful sometimes, but that's just the nature of the work. It's nothing new or out of the ordinary. And outside of work, no, there hasn't been any big trauma or anything since he moved in. Just the day-to-day stuff, and dealing with him being in the house.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this constant irritation and short-temperedness since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months now. Pretty much ever since he moved into our house, that's when it started.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding yourself avoiding certain activities or tasks at home because of your father-in-law's presence?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding things at home exactly, but it has affected some of my other responsibilities. I'm still doing my regular EMT shifts just fine. But... I've actually fallen behind on some paramedic coursework I was taking. And, uh, I usually help out with these community first-aid workshops, but I've stopped showing up for those consistently lately. It's just hard to focus with everything going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family members outside of your household since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My relationships outside the house are pretty much the same. I still talk to my friends and my side of the family like normal. It's really just the tension at home that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your spouse since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My wife and I are still doing okay. We still talk and get along the same as before. The issue is really just between me and him, not between me and her.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or activities at home that you're doing less often because of your father-in-law's presence?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a little bit ago, not really. I'm not doing anything less around the house itself. I still do my normal chores and whatever needs getting done... it's just tense. But his being there hasn't actually stopped me from doing what I need to do at home. It's really just the paramedic coursework and those community workshops I told you about that have dropped off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your father-in-law moving in was the main trigger for how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Honestly, that's exactly why I came in. Ever since he moved in with us... I mean, I'm holding it together at work just fine, but at home and with my coursework, I'm just a lot more wound up and short than I'm used to being. And I've started skipping the things that usually keep my head clear, like fishing, woodworking, and those first-aid workshops. So, yeah, it really all traces back to him moving in.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to do with your father-in-law that you no longer do because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: No, not really. We didn't really do much together before he moved in anyway. I mean, we'd see him for holidays or family dinners, but we didn't have shared hobbies or anything like that. So there isn't really anything we used to do that I've stopped doing... it's just the day-to-day of him being in the house all the time now that's getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any places or situations you're avoiding because of your father-in-law?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I'm not avoiding any specific places or anything like that. I mean, I still go home every day, even though it's stressful. I'm just avoiding my own hobbies and the coursework, like I said, but I'm not staying away from the house or anywhere else because of him.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven't been going fishing, or doing my woodworking, or even attending those first-aid workshops I usually like. I've just been skipping them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or empty. It's not like that. It's more just... frustration, I guess. I'm not really worrying about anything specific, or feeling nervous. Like I said, it's just this constant irritation. Pretty much ever since he moved into our house, that's when it started.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding yourself avoiding certain activities or tasks at home because of your father-in-law's presence?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding things at home exactly, but it has affected some of my other responsibilities. The issue is really just between me and him, not between me and her.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or activities at home that you're doing less often because of your father-in-law's presence?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a little bit ago, not really. I'm not doing anything less around the house itself. I still do my normal chores and whatever needs getting done... it's just tense. But his being there hasn't actually stopped me from doing what I need to do at home. It's really just the paramedic coursework and those community workshops I told you about that have dropped off.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your father-in-law moving in was the main trigger for how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, I'm holding it together at work just fine, but at home and with my coursework, I'm just a lot more wound up and short than I'm used to being. I'm not avoiding any specific places or anything like that. I mean, I still go home every day, even though it's stressful. I'm just avoiding my own hobbies and the coursework, like I said, but I'm not staying away from the house or anywhere else because of him.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of irritation and short-temperedness stayed the same since they started, or have they been getting better or worse over time?\n\nPatient: I'd say they've stayed pretty much the same. It hasn't gotten any worse, but it definitely hasn't gotten any better, either. It's just a constant level of frustration since he got here.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant impact on your daily life or overall functioning due to these feelings of irritation and short-temperedness?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still doing my regular EMT work just fine, so that hasn't changed. But... I have fallen behind on some of the paramedic coursework I was doing. I just don't have the patience or energy to focus on it when I get home. And I usually help out with these community first-aid workshops, but I've stopped showing up for those consistently. I just kind of tap out when I have the chance now.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt numb or detached from things that would normally upset or annoy you since your father-in-law moved in?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel numb or detached. So, no, not numb at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're having trouble coping with the stress of your father-in-law moving in?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I am. Honestly, that's why I came in.
